  SPIL (ASE subsidiary) broke ground on a ~NT$100bn, 6-hectare Douliu, Yunlin fab that will run CoWoS advanced packaging, phase one operational 2028, bringing SPIL's two-year investment to ~NT$200bn and pairing with the Huwei site (started Nov 2022, opened Sept 2025) as a twin Central Taiwan advanced-packaging base. The concrete signal for the cowos question is that merchant OSAT CoWoS capacity is being built at scale outside TSMC's own fabs — implying that by 2028 the CoWoS bottleneck migrates from interposer/assembly floorspace toward upstream constraints (HBM supply, ABF substrate, hybrid-bonding tools), which is directionally supportive of accelerator unit volumes for NVDA/AMD/AVGO/MRVL customers and of tool demand at AMAT/BESI/ONTO/CAMT. The article notes packaging is shifting from standardized back-end to 2.5D/3D IC and heterogeneous integration, raising advanced packaging's share of OSAT value — i.e., mix-driven margin expansion for ASE/SPIL rather than just volume. Timing is the caveat: 2028 first phase means nothing for 2026–27 supply; confirmation would be equipment orders or a pulled-forward phase-one date, refutation would be a customer-commitment-free build.
  Sources: [www.ctee.com.tw](https://www.ctee.com.tw/news/20260812700088-439901)
- `7` **台積董事會拍板 第2季配息7元 - 上市櫃 - 旺得富理財網**
  TSMC's Aug 11 board approved a US$29.4425bn capital budget tranche for advanced process, advanced packaging and fab construction, a NT$7/share Q2 dividend (unchanged — ex-date Dec 10, record Dec 16, paid Jan 7, with USD election newly offered to foreign holders), and up to ¥282bn for a Sony Semiconductor JV to develop next-gen image sensors targeting robotics and autonomous driving. Q2 results: revenue ~NT$1,270.4bn, net income ~NT$706.6bn. For tsmc-capex, the $29.4bn approval is a board authorization tranche, not a raise to the full-year guide — management had already lifted 2026 capex to as much as $64bn at the prior call, so this is consistent with, not incremental to, that guidance; treat any read of 'capex hike' as unsupported by this text. The unchanged NT$7 dividend disappointed retail per the linked coverage, which matters for the sell-the-beat framing: TSMC is choosing reinvestment over shareholder return, consistent with the CoWoS/SoIC capacity CAGRs cited elsewhere. The Sony JV is a new vector — image sensors for robots/AVs — small at ¥282bn but a signal that TSMC is buying into edge-AI sensing rather than only datacenter logic.
  Sources: [wantrich.chinatimes.com](https://wantrich.chinatimes.com/news/20260812900068-420101)
- `7` **Samsung ElectroMechanics' customer...｜Samsung Electro-Mechanics, glass substrate - ChainCatcher**
  The Elec reports Samsung Electro-Mechanics' glass-substrate program has slipped again: GlaSSEM (JV with Dongyu Fine Chem) has failed to give equipment order guidance to partners after postponing orders at least three times (Dec 2025 → March → June 2026), with no schedule communicated after June, because global comms-semi customers have not passed reliability certification on prototypes built at the Sejong pilot line. Mass-production targets have already moved from 2H27 (set at JV formation last month) to 2028 on the last earnings call, and the report implies a further slip since prototypes must be remade and requalified. Read-through: glass core is not a near-term substitute for ABF/organic substrates, which supports the abf-hanwha and substrate-supply theses (tight ABF through 2027+) and pushes out glass-tool revenue for the structuring/laser-drilling suppliers — LPKF is the covered name most levered to glass-core equipment orders, and this is a negative timing datapoint, not a technology cancellation. Note deposition/etch/laser-drill/inspection vendors are already selected, so an order burst is possible once reliability passes; confirmation would be GlaSSEM issuing an equipment order schedule or a named customer qual pass, refutation would be another silent quarter into 2027.
  Sources: [www.chaincatcher.com](https://www.chaincatcher.com/en/article/2282266) · [x.com](https://x.com/jukan05/status/2087415382638490041)
- `7` **Por qué Samsung y SK Hynix se disparan pese a un Nasdaq más débil**
  UBS's Timothy Arcuri raised HBM ASP growth to ~+79% YoY from a prior +67%, saying HBM4/HBM4E pricing is 'even stronger than we previously expected,' while Korean semiconductor exports rose 155.4% YoY to $9.95bn in the first 10 days of August (46.8% of all exports); Samsung rose >7% and SK Hynix >5% in Seoul even as the Nasdaq fell 0.6%. Goldman and Morgan Stanley both called the recent memory selloff overdone, with MS framing current valuations as a tactical re-entry point. Crucially, UBS argues Nvidia's reported de-spec of Rubin Ultra (less HBM per accelerator due to shortage) is demand-accretive, not dilutive — fewer stacks per GPU allows more GPUs shipped, raising total HBM consumption. For 000660 this is the cleanest positive datapoint (highest HBM leverage), and it links rubin-ramp to hbm4: the read is refuted if suppliers add supply faster than demand and ASPs roll, or if Nvidia's config change cuts total stack demand more than the unit uplift compensates — watch September contract HBM settlements and Nvidia's Rubin Ultra BOM confirmation.
  Sources: [invezz.com](https://invezz.com/es/noticias/2026/08/12/por-que-samsung-y-sk-hynix-se-disparan-pese-a-un-nasdaq-mas-debil)
- `7` **Le ultimissime del 13 agosto – Dalian, Huawei e crisi memorie**
  At KeyBanc (Aug 10) Micron's Sumit Sadana said the company can serve less than half of data-center demand and that customers now name DRAM — not power, not shell space, not logic wafers — as the #1 build constraint, with 2027 expected tighter than 2026; 16 take-or-pay Strategic Customer Agreements, mostly extended to 2030, cover roughly half of revenue, though Micron itself flags list-price increases are decelerating as it optimizes mix/volume/price (spot stays soft: DDR4 +0.93%, bid-ask gap persists). Second, HBM4E starts the custom-HBM era with customer-co-developed SKUs whose qual cost/time makes three-vendor sourcing per program unlikely — implying a dual- or single-supplier structure per accelerator, which raises both the prize and the risk for 000660 and Samsung; Micron moves 1-beta→1-gamma at HBM4E and buys standard and custom logic dies from TSMC, extending TSM's grip to the logic layer of the HBM stack. Third, on nand-cycle/wfe-china: SK hynix restarted Dalian Fab 2 (Solidigm), building complete, tool-in possible from November, MP 1H27, ~50k wpm on top of Fab 1's 100k (+50% China capacity) but capped at ~100-layer floating-gate because of US EUV/advanced-tool restrictions, while >300-layer goes to Cheongju M17 (KRW19.1tn, first cleanroom by end-2028); Samsung's Xi'an finished V6→V8 (236L) on Mar 30 and preps V9 (286L) for 2027. Risk framing worth holding: Xi'an is 30–35% of Samsung NAND and Dalian 35–40% of SK hynix NAND, and both sites are qualifying AMEC Chinese etch tools — an explicit second-order export-control datapoint (share shift from US WFE to Naura/AMEC at Korean-owned China fabs) that would hurt LRCX/AMAT China revenue if it scales. Samsung HBM4 yields ~80% means the 2H earnings swing factor is price, not yield.
  Sources: [elettronicanews.it](https://elettronicanews.it/micron-memoria-sk-hynix-dalian-huawei-kirin)
- `7` **Anthropic investors target $2 trillion IPO valuation in October**
  Anthropic backers are targeting a ~$2T IPO valuation in October on expected annualized revenue of $100–120B by year-end 2026, described as more than 10x the level at the start of 2026 — a growth rate that, if real, is the single largest new source of frontier-lab compute demand outside OpenAI. The text gives no compute or capex detail, so the read-through is inferential: a lab scaling revenue ~10x in a year implies training/inference capacity commitments that flow to NVDA, the neocloud complex (NBIS, CRWV, IREN), and — given Anthropic's known Trainium exposure — to the mrvl-trainium and openai-xpu custom-silicon questions where MRVL/AVGO share is contested. Treat the $100–120B figure as investor-sourced marketing, not audited; confirmation would be an S-1 with disclosed revenue and purchase obligations, or a named multi-year capacity deal with a hyperscaler. Refutation would be an IPO delay or a valuation cut, which would also cool the sentiment premium in the neocloud names. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [qz.com](https://qz.com/anthropic-ipo-2-trillion-valuation-october-081326)
- `6` **SPIL breaks ground on advanced IC packaging, testing plant in Yunlin - Focus Taiwan**
  Same groundbreaking, with the financing and customer detail: SPIL's Douliu CoWoS plant is NT$100bn (US$3.11bn), 6 hectares, phase one in 2028, 2,200 jobs, and the Huwei plant opened Sept 2025 is already in commercial production and generating profit — a useful proof point that the prior CoWoS capacity tranche is ramping profitably rather than sitting idle. Funding is being topped up via parallel rights issues at both SPIL and ASE of 809.7m shares at NT$20 (NT$16.19bn each, ~NT$32.4bn combined) explicitly for production materials, with ASE Technology subscribing in full — the 'materials' earmark suggests substrate/interposer input pre-buying, which reads as a tightening signal for ABF substrate supply (abf-hanwha) rather than for equipment. MOEA also confirms SPIL is a named partner in AMD's >$100bn Taiwan AI supply-chain program announced in May alongside ASE and TSMC, tying this capacity directly to AMD's MI455X/Helios ramp rather than to Nvidia alone. If AMD volumes disappoint, this capacity becomes generic CoWoS supply and pressures 2028 packaging pricing; confirmation of the AMD linkage would be SPIL disclosing customer concentration or a second rights issue for tooling.

- `6` **TSMC Reports Up to 99% Yield for 5.5-Reticle CoWoS Packaging**
  TSMC disclosed production yields above 98% and up to 99% on selected 5.5-reticle CoWoS products, with a roadmap to a 15-reticle platform by 2029 (note: Chinese-language reports of the same OCP talk say 14x — sources disagree on the multiple, agree on 2029). The decision-useful point is that CoWoS output is now gated by installed capacity and upstream components, not by assembly yield loss — high yield converts every available logic die and HBM stack into sellable accelerators, which raises the marginal value of incremental HBM supply (000660, MU, 285A) and of CoWoS floorspace additions (SPIL/ASE, item above). Second, TSMC flags that customers are dual-sourcing ABF substrates and that substrates from different suppliers have different mechanical/electrical characteristics, so a design tuned to one material set can lose system-level yield — that is a direct qualification barrier favoring incumbent substrate vendors and a hurdle for new entrants (abf-hanwha), and it explains the shift from device-level to system-level validation (read-through to test/inspection demand at ONTO, CAMT, TER, Advantest). Refutation of the 'yield is solved' read would be any evidence that larger 5.5x+ packages degrade yield materially as reticle multiples scale toward 2029.
  Sources: [www.guru3d.com](https://www.guru3d.com/story/tsmc-reports-up-to-yield-for-55reticle-cowos-packaging)
- `6` **AI demand drives triple-digit profit growth for Chinese chip foundries SMIC, Hua Hong | South China Morning Post**
  SMIC Q2 net profit $479.2M (+261.7% y/y) on revenue $3.0B (+36% y/y, vs $2.9B Bloomberg consensus); Hua Hong net $38.6M (+385.9%) on record revenue $717.5M (+26.8% vs $702.7M consensus) — the profit leverage is far ahead of the revenue beat, which implies utilization and mix (domestic AI accelerators shielded from US export controls) are repricing mature-node capacity rather than volume alone. SMIC explicitly guided to persistent AI 'spillover' demand in 2H and said it will 'accelerate new capacity to ease supply constraints' — a stated capacity-add intent, which is the read-through for wfe-china (Naura, AMEC, Skyverse) and for the Huawei Ascend 950PR/950DT supply chain that depends on SMIC wafer starts. For AMAT/LRCX/KLA/ONTO the sign is ambiguous: China expansion is demand, but entity-list scope determines whether US toolmakers capture it, so treat this as a domestic-WFE datapoint until SMIC quantifies capex. Confirms if SMIC raises FY capex guidance and Naura/AMEC book orders next quarter; refutes if the margin surge proves ASP/one-off driven with flat capex.

- `6` **Cloudflare Is Now Blocking AI Bots by Default. Here’s What It Means for Your Traffic**
  Cloudflare will block AI training and agent bots by default from 15 September 2026, while continuing to allow search crawlers. Thin sourcing, but the mechanism matters for net-act4: default-deny converts crawler access into a negotiated/paid transaction at Cloudflare's edge, strengthening the pay-per-crawl and x402 monetization rails and making NET a toll collector on agentic web traffic rather than just a CDN. Watch for named licensing deals or disclosed pay-per-crawl revenue to confirm; a carve-out list that exempts the major labs would refute the pricing-power read. *(headline only)*

  ComputerBase's breakdown of the same Counterpoint dataset gives the full Q2 2026 share table by units: Samsung 25% (-4pts QoQ), SK hynix incl. Solidigm 22% (+4pts), YMTC 14% (from 13% in Q1), Kioxia ~14%, Micron 13% (flat), Sandisk 11% (-2pts) — Sandisk is the clear share donor and now the smallest of the big six. Counterpoint attributes Samsung's decline to deliberate reallocation toward higher-margin DRAM/HBM, which is a supply-discipline positive for NAND pricing even as it flatters YMTC's relative position. For SNDK the datapoint cuts against the investor-day growth narrative: losing 2pts of unit share in a quarter is consistent with capital-light capacity discipline and NBM allocation, but only if ASP/mix gains more than offset it. - `5` **Goldman Sachs just answered the biggest question about AI | Louis Velazquez - Official Website, Entrepreneur, Finance, Technology**
  Goldman quantifies how the buildout is being paid for: record $252B of US equity issuance in Q2 (topping the $234B Q1-2021 record), with AI-related companies ~40% of YTD follow-on volume ($105B through July, highest since 2021); the four largest hyperscalers spend ~$725B of capex in 2026, up ~77% from $410B in 2025 and roughly equal to their entire combined operating cash flow, with capex expected to outpace operating cash flow by a wide margin in 2027 and credit strategists projecting >1/3 of 2027 capex debt-funded. The concrete evidence is Alphabet: FY26 capex guide raised to $195–205B from $180–190B, first negative free cash flow (-$5.9B) since the 2004 IPO as $44.9B quarterly capex outran $39.1B operating cash, and zero buybacks vs $13.2B a year earlier. For hyperscaler-guides this is the inflection-point datapoint: the marginal dollar of AI capex is now financed by credit markets, so the entire coverage chain (NVDA, AVGO, MRVL, VRT, CRWV, NBIS, IREN, memory) acquires a new sensitivity to IG spreads and bond-market appetite that did not exist when capex was self-funded. Confirms if 2027 hyperscaler bond issuance lands in the hundreds of billions as projected; refutes if operating cash flow inflects up on AI revenue and buybacks resume.
  Sources: [louisvelazquez.com](https://louisvelazquez.com/goldman-sachs-just-answered-the-biggest-question-about-ai)
- `5` **Intuit Adds AI Chat to QuickBooks, Enterprise Suite | INTU Stock News**
  Intuit expanded Intuit Intelligence across QuickBooks Online Advanced and Intuit Enterprise Suite, headlined by Intuit Intelligence Chat — conversational query, anomaly surfacing, report generation and workflow initiation over the customer's own data, with actions executed only after user confirmation; IES adds multi-entity accounting, multi-currency and deeper industry workflows to push up-market. Intuit's own 2026 Future of Finance Report claims high-growth multi-entity finance leaders are >2x more likely to use AI in finance workflows than slower-growth peers, which is the demand argument for attach. For the intu-agentic bear-watch, this is defensive product news rather than a resolution: the moat being asserted is proprietary data plus auditability ('AI they can stand behind when asked how a number was reached'), which is precisely the ground a general-purpose Claude/Gemini/ChatGPT bookkeeping agent cannot easily occupy — that framing suggests Intuit is positioning the audit trail, not the model, as the defensible layer. No pricing or attach disclosed, so nothing here quantifies revenue; confirmation would be disclosed IES/Advanced ARPC uplift or seat growth at the next print, refutation would be a frontier lab shipping a bank-connected bookkeeping agent with accountant-grade review workflows.
  Sources: [stocktitan.net](https://stocktitan.net/news/INTU/intuit-advances-its-mid-market-platform-with-conversational-ai-0urm7porjjgj.html)
- `4` **Intel contempla volver al mercado de la memoria ante la creciente demanda de la IA**
  Intel CEO Lip-Bu Tan, on the TechSurge podcast, said Intel is researching new memory architectures and 3D stacking, framing memory as no longer a low-margin commodity given genAI training/inference demand — the first explicit signal of a possible return since the 2022 Optane/3D XPoint shutdown. The concrete corroborating fact is personnel: Seok-Hee Lee, former SK Hynix CEO, is now EVP of Intel's Foundry and Advanced Packaging division; the reported XBM/ZAM architectures targeting ~2x HBM4 bandwidth are dated 2029–2030 and Tan explicitly said Intel is not ready to detail plans. For coverage this is long-dated optionality for INTC (intel-18a/foundry question) and no threat to 000660/MU HBM pricing power inside the investable horizon; the more actionable angle is that a memory-experienced executive sits over advanced packaging, which suggests Intel wants HBM-adjacent stacking/EMIB-Foveros attach rather than a DRAM fab. Treat product specs as rumor-tier until Intel presents a roadmap; confirmation would be a named memory partner or capex line item, refutation is continued 'not ready to discuss'.
  Sources: [profesionalreview.com](https://profesionalreview.com/2026/08/13/intel-mercado-memoria-creciente-demanda-ia)
- `4` **Chinas YMTC ist drittgrößter NAND-Flash-Hersteller der Welt | heise online**
  Heise adds the granular figures: YMTC jumped from 6th to 3rd in one year with production volume up 22% YoY, making it the world's largest pure-play NAND maker (Samsung and SK hynix/Solidigm also make DRAM), while ranking only 5th on revenue — implying materially lower ASPs and a consumer-weighted mix versus the eSSD-led market, where enterprise SSDs are already 48% of shipments and should cross 50% by year-end. Trendforce Q1 revenue for reference: Samsung $13.5bn NAND, SK hynix+Solidigm $7.5bn, Kioxia/Micron/Sandisk ~$6bn each, YMTC ~$4bn — and prices have risen sharply since, with margins on both NAND and DRAM reportedly beyond 80%. Two things follow for coverage: NAND pricing power for SNDK is currently intact because the marginal Chinese bit is not competing for eSSD sockets, and YMTC's pending IPO will force disclosure of finances/capex for the first time — the single best future dataset for sizing the nand-cycle oversupply risk. Heise also notes Chinese memory prices are not cheaper domestically, which argues the shortage is global rather than an allocation artifact.
  Sources: [heise.de](https://heise.de/news/Chinas-YMTC-ist-drittgroesster-NAND-Flash-Hersteller-der-Welt-11413360.html)
- `4` **Data Center IT Semiconductors and Components Market to Surpass $1.8 Trillion over Next Five Years, According to Dell'Oro Group**
  Dell'Oro now sees data center IT semis and components reaching $1.8T by 2030, revised significantly upward from its January 2026 report on higher data center capex and power capacity assumptions — the revision, not the level, is the news. Three specifics matter for coverage: (1) 'supply constraints are expected to sustain elevated pricing for memory and storage' near-term before moderating later in the forecast, which supports the 000660/MU/SNDK/285A pricing-power thesis for the next several quarters but explicitly builds in normalization — a caution on terminal-value memory multiples; (2) accelerators, CPUs and NICs 'will increasingly shift toward custom silicon as hyperscalers seek greater efficiency and lower costs,' the core MRVL/AVGO share-shift-vs-NVDA call; (3) NIC growth accelerating on both front-end (general-purpose inference/agentic/storage servers) and back-end scale-out, which is a direct MRVL/AVGO/NET-adjacent networking datapoint. Also flags >200GW of server/storage power draw over five years — read-through to VRT and power-chain names. This is a forecast revision, so it confirms direction rather than proving it; watch actual hyperscaler custom-ASIC unit disclosures and DRAM contract prices for validation.
  Sources: [prnewswire.com](https://prnewswire.com/news-releases/data-center-it-semiconductors-and-components-market-to-surpass-1-8-trillion-over-next-five-years-according-to-delloro-group-302849183.html)
- `2` **Meta Stock Resumes Uptrend Toward $600 as Facebook’s Advertising Cash Cow Fights Massive AI CapEx - Forex News by FX Leaders**
  Recap of Meta Q2: revenue $60.80B (+28% y/y) on +14% impressions and +12% price per ad, ARPP $16.86 — the ad engine is not the problem. The problem is the funding math: quarterly capex $31.08B, FY guide floor raised to $130–145B, quarterly FCF collapsed to $784M and operating margin compressed 43%→31% y/y. For meta-capex the signal is that Meta is now spending essentially all of its incremental cash flow, which is demand-positive for NVDA/VRT/CRWV/ARM-adjacent buildout names but removes the cushion — any ad-growth stumble forces a capex choice rather than a buyback cut. Note this is a technical/opinion piece, not new disclosure; treat the numbers as recap. Confirms if Meta guides FY2027 capex above the $145B run-rate at next print; refutes if FCF recovers while capex holds, implying depreciation timing rather than structural strain.

- `7` **Nebius Says AI Compute Prices Jumped 15%, Signaling Strong Pricing Power**
  Nebius' first capacity auction cleared 15% above the highest price the company had ever charged for Blackwell-generation capacity, per CEO Arkady Volozh — a real-time, market-clearing measurement of willingness to pay rather than a list-price announcement. This is the cleanest available rebuttal to the GPU-rental-deflation/short-depreciation-life bear case that has been used against CRWV, IREN and, second-order, NVDA's sell-through durability: if marginal capacity clears higher than legacy contracts, realized ASPs on new bookings should rise as capacity lands, supporting revenue per MW without proportional capex. Caveats stated in the source: one auction is not a trend, existing contracts are not repriced, and margin uplift is not guaranteed. Confirmation would be a second auction clearing at or above this level, or disclosed revenue-per-MW rising q/q at NBIS/CRWV; refutation would be Rubin-generation supply arriving and collapsing Blackwell spot pricing (ties to rubin-ramp).
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMi2AFBVV95cUxNYU9weHk4THk2Tl9ENk5aWEZiTjRwRmF3X3RJSnJMMDFBRUpnTXhCdXdHT2dJVzVIbDJPMDRRMnExNVpRWnF4dl9wVnZOY2c4TF9XZ284cFV3THRwbkhza1NndHFpVDdsV1lVZ3pNTDZmbEstLVpoMmwwNTFraHk2UVZsMFRtd0Y5MEt5X25UeW9WaDVnSnE5ZDRlM0FyQkdwNHF0b1Jqb3FZZTNtYllIOF9XRF9FQmttS1hyaG9rVTFQOEZWUExtbEUyVGsyY21wZTRoRU5Wdjk?oc=5) · [benzinga.com](https://www.benzinga.com/trading-ideas/long-ideas/26/08/61187906/nebius-says-ai-compute-prices-jumped-15-percent-after-first-capacity-auction)
- `7` **Cheniere Energy (LNG) Q2 2026 Earnings Call Transcript | The Motley Fool** → [[Theses/LNG - Cheniere Energy]]
  Cheniere raised FY26 EBITDA guidance to $7.9–8.4B from $7.25–7.75B (~+$625M at the midpoint) and DCF to $5.3–5.8B (+$550M midpoint), on Q2 adjusted EBITDA of $1.8B (+27% y/y), DCF $1.17B, and 184 cargoes/672 TBtu (+20%); full-year production was raised and tightened to 53–54Mt from 52–54Mt. The mechanism is geopolitical, not structural: ~18Mt of lost Middle East supply through the Strait of Hormuz plus an 11 bcm European storage deficit (~100 cargoes) lifted marketing margins, while Feygin warned Europe will "struggle to reach the 80% storage target before winter" — bullish for spot but a risk if a Hormuz normalization compresses margins. Downside is largely fenced: less than 1Mt of open 2026 volume remains and every $1 change in market margin is worth under $50M of EBITDA, so the raise is mostly banked. Watch Train 7 first LNG (imminent as of the Aug 6 call), CCL Stage 3 at 98.4% and Trains 8/9 at 48.3% (ahead of schedule), the $4.7B Bechtel EPC for SPL Phase 1 (>6 mtpa including 1 Mt from BOG reliquefaction), and the offsetting demand signal of China H1 imports −10% y/y to ~27Mt against 38Mt of new global FIDs YTD — the medium-term supply-glut argument against LNG.
  Sources: [fool.com](https://fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/08/13/cheniere-energy-lng-q2-2026-earnings-call-transcript)
- `7` **Lumentum Q4: Everything Screams Sell, Except The Numbers**
  Lumentum guided the September quarter to a $4.20 EPS midpoint — per this author, 2.04x what LITE earned across all of FY2025 — and June-quarter revenue rose $525.6m YoY, of which Components contributed $329.0m versus Systems' $196.6m, i.e. the growth is in chips (EMLs, lasers) not finished boxes. The $5.0bn FY2027 revenue figure (+66%) is the author's model holding the September guide flat for four quarters, not company guidance — treat it as an arithmetic floor argument, not a disclosure. Mechanism: datacenter optics demand is pulling merchant laser/component capacity, which is the same InP/EML bottleneck behind the inp-capacity and china-optics questions and a leading indicator for AAOI, IQE (InP epiwafer pull-through) and CPO timing; component-led mix normally carries better incremental margin than systems, consistent with the EPS step-function. Confirm on the earnings call with disclosed InP wafer-start expansion, Coherent/Veeco MOCVD orders, or a named 800G/1.6T customer ramp; refute if the guide leans on one-time systems shipments or if EML lead times shorten, which would signal the shortage rent is temporary. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [seekingalpha.com](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4936257-lumentum-q4-everything-screams-sell-except-the-numbers)
- `6` **SK hynix becomes Kioxia effective largest shareholder** → [[Theses/285A - Kioxia]]
  SK hynix has become Kioxia's effective largest shareholder after Toshiba's stake fell from 14.48% (July 31) to 14.12%, leaving Bain vehicle BCPE Pangea Cayman 2 — into which SK hynix holds convertible bonds — on top; SK hynix is contractually capped at ≤15% voting in Kioxia through 2028 absent Kioxia consent, so no operational control today. The strategic point for nand-cycle: the world's #2 and #3 NAND makers are now linked at the cap table just as the industry runs tight, which structurally biases toward supply discipline rather than share-grab capacity adds. This touches 000660 (optionality on NAND consolidation, plus continued HBM cash generation) and SNDK, whose fab JV is with Kioxia — any move by SK hynix toward direct control would put SanDisk's JV governance and bit-supply arrangements in play. Watch for merger/investment filings in Japan, Korea, the US and EU; the article explicitly flags multi-jurisdiction review as the gating item, so absent those filings this remains symbolic rather than actionable.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMilgFBVV95cUxPTjQ1bWZjX1I3bHJZdjh1M2x2SUJWNmJiZ3UtWlhqTVozTlNGSG1zUU1FZGlxamlhSGx3Qmp0SWZTamQyQ2ExRElkS0lPSWpBMXhKeEY0OEhSU0VQaWNLOXdhRWlTQUxjbHZfVHdUSXVuc1NNUVh6bHBnQjZMUk12MDkyS3FERXdudjF3QXRLd09feV9oYnfSAZsBQVVfeXFMTzVkODB0a1hfVmNWd0E1SVNPSENsVUJmcjlQT3lGQU1EcnRZMnVDTUQ4TnhGWUUyZENYc3RQbEdFaHBsMEtqQVFtMEhrRy13a1RpY0pxSjd5NHd3bXZSRU5wQ1VvZFJXZ1k2VW9kUHNjbGxnaVQ4d3FWSXdqNmI5aElJeGF4a0NHM081NHNva1pDeGFsUHQ0b0pmZDQ?oc=5) · [upi.com](https://www.upi.com/Top_News/World-News/2026/08/11/sk-hynix-kioxia-chipmaker/1161786491675/)
- `6` **SK Hynix Faces a Two-Front Test: Groundbreaking in Indiana, Bargaining Table at Home** → [[Theses/000660 - SK Hynix]]
  The inflection datapoint here is pricing, not capex: TrendForce now models DRAM ASPs +~20% q/q in 3Q26, doubled from its prior +10% call, with HBM4 shipments accelerating in 2H — a mid-cycle upward revision that directly feeds 000660's margin leverage and cuts against Morningstar's July 30 fair-value cut (-8% to KRW2.2m) premised on softer memory prices. Against that, SK Hynix is spending into the cycle: $3.87bn West Lafayette advanced-packaging/HBM fab breaking ground Aug 27 with mass production only in 2H28, KRW54tn board-approved for Yongin Y2 and Cheongju M17, and a Solidigm Dalian Fab 2 retool through end-2026 lifting NAND output 50% from 1H27 — an explicit split of HBM/packaging to the US and commodity NAND to China that hedges export-control risk but adds 2027 NAND supply (a negative datapoint for nand-cycle and for SNDK/285A pricing durability). Stock +5.9% to KRW1,593,000 (+6.6% w/w) yet still 47% below its 52-week high, with RBC pegging HBM share ~55% and Cantor/Rosenblatt initiating at $300/$320; Chairman Chey's July 30 open-market buy of 3,620 shares is a confidence signal. Two swing factors to track: the newly formed employee representative body forming mid-wage-round (cost pressure on the Y2/M17 return math, unpriced per the article) and the OCP high-bandwidth-flash standard published with SanDisk plus a possible Solidigm NASDAQ listing — the HBF standard is the item that most directly touches SNDK's hbf-flash thesis. Confirmation of the pricing read would be 3Q26 contract prints landing near +20%; refutation would be TrendForce walking the number back or HBM4 qualification slipping.
  Sources: [www.ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/unternehmensnachrichten/sk-hynix-faces-a-two-front-test-groundbreaking-in-indiana-bargaining/69947065)
- `6` **Chinese firm tops Micron and Kioxia in shipments of NAND memory chips** → [[Theses/285A - Kioxia]]
  Counterpoint data (released Wed) put YMTC third globally in Q2 2026 NAND shipments at 14% share, passing Micron and Kioxia, with Director MS Hwang projecting YMTC 'to pull further ahead in 2027 and 2028' and noting 15% share is the minimum needed to self-fund capex — i.e. YMTC is about one quarter from capex self-sufficiency despite export controls. Context: CXMT already holds 7% of DRAM (4th, behind Micron/SK hynix/Samsung) after its blockbuster mainland IPO last month, and YMTC is now preparing its own Shanghai listing; DRAM and NAND both set records in Q1 at ~$100bn and $46bn. The offset for SNDK/MU bulls is that YMTC still trails on revenue because its mix skews consumer rather than data center, a segment expected to absorb half of all NAND by end-2026 — so near-term eSSD pricing power is intact, but the bit-supply overhang is structural. Confirmation of the bear case would be YMTC qualifying at a hyperscaler for eSSD; refutation is YMTC staying capped at ~100-layer-class consumer product, as SK hynix's parallel Dalian constraint suggests China-based lines can add volume but not technology.
  Sources: - `6` **SK Hynix is spending $720bn as its shares fall by half** → [[Theses/SKM - SK Telecom]]
  The decision-relevant number here is not the $720bn capex headline but TrendForce's pricing curve: conventional DRAM contract prices +58–63% in Q2 decelerating to just +13–18% for server DRAM in Q3 — the second derivative rolling over is exactly the mechanism that de-rates memory equities without any price decline, and it explains SK Hynix −50% and Samsung −34% from June highs despite both posting record earnings in the window. Directly on 000660 (and MU, with SNDK/285A on the NAND leg given the Cheongju NAND build): the bull case now rests on contract structure rather than spot — 10 long-term agreements at SK Hynix, 16 at Micron as of end-June, Samsung targeting 60–70% of planned capacity under multi-year deals — plus Nvidia's $500bn SK Group arrangement covering HBM supply, co-developed next-gen memory and SK Telecom datacenters by 2027. Chey Tae-won's framing that Nvidia wants custom chips and Google wants customized HBM "changes the memory chip's status" is the pushback on cyclicality and ties to the hbm4/samsung-hbm questions; his concession that prices "went up too fast" cuts the other way. Confirming datapoints: Q3 shareholder-return announcement (SK Hynix has set a Q3 timeline), Yongin fab 1 production start in February, and whether Q4 server DRAM contract growth stabilizes above ~15%; refuting: further LTA signings that lock pricing would decouple the equity from spot. The quiet sale process for the Chongqing packaging plant is a small export-control/geopolitics tell worth tracking.
  Sources: [thenextweb.com](https://thenextweb.com/news/sk-hynix-720bn-memory-fabs-yongin-peak-memory)
- `6` **TSMC Stock Rises 2.2% as $4.69 Billion Sony Venture Launches — TradingView News** → [[Theses/TSM - Taiwan Semiconductor]]
  TSMC and Sony formed Advanced Vision Semiconductor Manufacturing, a ¥747bn (~$4.69bn) image-sensor JV in Kumamoto, with Sony contributing ¥465bn (~$2.92bn) and TSMC ¥282bn (~38%); Sony controls the venture and owns sensor technology and design, TSMC supplies process and volume manufacturing, first target is smartphone sensors, and volume production is targeted for 2029 with capital deployed in stages depending on demand. For the tsmc-capex question this is mildly positive on structure rather than scale: it extends the Japanese footprint and diversifies into specialty at only ~38% funding, with future tranches contingent on Japanese government support — i.e., it does not compete meaningfully with leading-edge/CoWoS capital allocation, and staged deployment limits the fixed-cost drag. The offsetting point the source makes is valuation: TSM at $430.41 sits 35.3% above the cited GF Value of $318.07 as of Aug 12, which sharpens the sell-the-beat risk into the hyperscaler capex guide window. Confirmation of the specialty thesis would be a disclosed capacity figure or anchor customer beyond Sony; refutation would be TSMC's 2029 start slipping or the Japanese subsidy failing to materialize.
  Sources: [www.tradingview.com](https://www.tradingview.com/news/gurufocus:ee7872ec7094b:0-tsmc-stock-rises-2-2-as-4-69-billion-sony-venture-launches/)
- `6` **AMD looks to raise $4 billion to $5 billion in debt offering, source says**
  AMD is marketing a $4–5bn debt raise, per a Reuters source; no use-of-proceeds, coupon, or tenor is given in the text. Size matters relative to AMD's historically near-debt-free balance sheet: a raise of this scale implies pre-funding of the MI450/Helios rack-scale buildout — HBM and CoWoS-class capacity prepayments, supply deposits, and possibly the equity/financing side of large 2026–27 customer deals — rather than routine refinancing (inference; the text states neither purpose nor terms). For amd-parity this is a capacity-commitment signal ahead of MLPerf/ROCm proof points: vendors get paid before parity is demonstrated, which is incrementally positive for HBM suppliers (000660, MU) and for TSM/CoWoS bookings. Confirm via the prospectus use-of-proceeds language and any disclosed supply prepayments in the next 10-Q; refute if proceeds are earmarked for refinancing or buybacks, which would make this a capital-structure story, not a demand datapoint. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [reuters.com](https://www.reuters.com/technology/amd-looks-raise-4-billion-5-billion-debt-offering-source-says-2026-08-13/)
- `5` **Oracle and AWS Deepen Strategic Collaboration as Enterprise Adoption of Oracle AI Database@AWS Accelerates**
  Oracle and AWS signed an expanded long-term strategic collaboration agreement to accelerate migrations to Oracle AI Database@AWS, now live in 22 AWS Regions one year after GA, and made Exadata Database Service on Exascale Infrastructure generally available — pooled storage with pay-per-use economics that extends Exadata down-market to smaller databases and multi-region HA/DR, plus instant thin cloning. Named production customers: CJ Olive Young, Kobalt Music Group, Metropolitan Transport Authority of Barcelona. For ORCL this is the capital-light half of the story — multicloud database revenue that carries none of the OCI GPU-buildout capex or lease-financing risk currently driving the bear case — and Exascale's lower entry point widens the addressable base, at the cost of potential ASP cannibalization of dedicated Exadata. Confirmation would be Oracle quantifying multicloud database revenue growth on the next earnings call; the pay-per-use shift is the metric that could cut either way.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMi5gFBVV95cUxNcE5vQW9nTWl1OVlic29HOXFaS0lldy1SRlVTbTV3RGpiQV9GSG9YQ21YaEZPMGRVYVQtOHVUSzNOR2M1WldIUmEySVdGazRKMmlEcW9zUUNZVDFiN2MyLUtESFJKVDU5M3FGME94azhXcy1MdzBReGhJWW1TbV9sZTBtUU9kR2t1UXpUUDl2ZGp4VXp4bktDMWY2Slh0eEdyZkVvRk1URXdfR0U4V2N6WkV3SW1Rc0NCTmJZNEppQXVpWEQ2dlBsZDFGTFNSZmZOQUJDUXc2cFNtbzlubEdzbWVRdjNvUQ?oc=5) · [prnewswire.com](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/oracle-and-aws-deepen-strategic-collaboration-as-enterprise-adoption-of-oracle-ai-databaseaws-accelerates-302850291.html)
- `5` **Uber Partners for Tokyo Robotaxi Pilot in Late 2026 | UBER Stock News** → [[Theses/UBER - Uber]]
  Uber Japan signed an operational agreement with Tokyo taxi operator Hinomaru Kotsu to run depot ops, cleaning, maintenance, charging and uptime for an AV pilot launching late 2026, executing the March 12, 2026 MOU with Wayve and Nissan (Nissan LEAF running Wayve's AI Driver). Critically, Japanese law requires an authorized taxi company to operate passenger transport, so Uber contributes only the matching platform and ops tools — and the initial phase keeps Hinomaru safety drivers behind the wheel, with fully driverless operation deferred pending regulatory approval. For UBER/uber-av this is the asset-light network playbook again: Uber captures demand aggregation without fleet capex, and the regulatory structure in Japan structurally favors a platform partner over a vertically integrated operator like Waymo, which implies Tokyo is a market Uber can hold rather than lose. Read it as incremental optionality, not a P&L event — late-2026 launch with safety operators means no driverless unit economics before 2027 at the earliest; confirmation would be a Japanese ministry approval for driverless operation or a stated fleet size, refutation would be slippage of the late-2026 date.
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMirAFBVV95cUxPZkVDcDg3ZkNPbkxhTnRlSVhCNEMzQ1hmY0I4d3NxclZhSWdnLUdwb2lsa3J2WGkzRFpLbXczbDVndXp2R01hUzNuZkxTMjJxX29HN0pKcHNEMjRSVG5CdV9QakozZldlWW1MZWlJUU5sZC1rOTFjbEVnT01EMFNTNUNBazZ0dlhGXzUtU0VKdWxDNXlBUFp2aVRpdllVTHgxQmQyNkZLRll2dF9O?oc=5) · [www.stocktitan.net](https://www.stocktitan.net/news/UBER/uber-partners-with-hinomaru-kotsu-for-robotaxi-pilot-deployment-in-z03w8xw1urha.html)
- `5` **Nebius Q2 Earnings: Transitioning From Massive Backlog To Explosive Cash Flow**
  Nebius reported $582m of quarterly revenue, +454% YoY, with $236m positive adjusted EBITDA and "own capital" of $10.34bn — the key shift is from signed-backlog narrative to actual EBITDA generation, which is the variable that determines whether neoclouds can self-fund GPU cohorts rather than dilute. The stated model is asset-light with customer prepayments funding expansion and debt growth contained, i.e. the opposite of the leveraged-neocloud risk profile the market applies to CRWV; the European sovereign/low-latency angle is a regulatory moat argument, which is the author's framing, not a disclosed contract term. For coverage this is a second-order demand confirmation for NVDA GPU sell-through and for the rack/power chain (VRT), and a read-across on neocloud utilization economics. Confirm via disclosed contracted backlog conversion rates and capex-to-revenue in the next print; refute if EBITDA positivity depends on capitalized costs or one-off customer prepayments rather than recurring compute rental. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [seekingalpha.com](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4935655-nebius-q2-earnings-transitioning-from-massive-backlog-to-explosive-cash-flow)
- `5` **Nvidia unveils first open-source AI model since CEO Jensen Huang entered the chat**
  Nvidia released Nemotron 3.5 Lightning, its first open-weight model since Huang's late-July X debut and open letter defending open weights, explicitly sized to run on a single GPU in a laptop/desktop and free to download, modify and commercialize; CrowdStrike, CodeRabbit and Harvey are named early testers, and Nvidia also shipped NeMo Switchyard for cheapest-path model routing. The strategic mechanism is stated by Huang himself — 'free AI should be great for chips' — i.e., commoditizing the model layer to depress token prices, expand usage, and route the value back to GPUs, while blunting the pricing power of closed labs (OpenAI/Anthropic) that are also Nvidia's biggest customers; the trigger context is Kimi K3 from Moonshot narrowing the gap and Washington's distillation/sanctions debate, plus Zuckerberg's parallel open-source manifesto and Meta's Muse Spark. For NVDA this is demand-defensive rather than a new revenue line (the model is free), and for META it corroborates the open-weight posture that underwrites Meta capex; CRWD getting named as a design partner alongside Nvidia's July cybersecurity consortium is a small credibility datapoint for agentic security workloads. Confirming evidence would be Nemotron download/derivative share on HuggingFace and enterprise agent deployments citing it; refuting evidence would be a single-GPU-class model wave that shifts inference to client silicon and edge NPUs rather than incremental datacenter GPU demand.
  Sources: [cnbc.com](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/11/nvidia-releases-nemotron-3point5-lightning-open-source-ai-model-.html)
- `4` **SK Hynix apuesta USD $720.000 millones: la mayor construcción de fábricas de memoria para IA - DiarioBitcoin** → [[Theses/SKM - SK Telecom]]
  Same SK Hynix story with the capital-markets detail: the July Nasdaq listing raised $26.5bn — the largest ever by a foreign issuer in US markets — and the ADR has since fallen ~21% from a ~$195 high on July 14 to $154.41, so new US holders are underwater even as market cap passed $1trn and the company held 58% HBM share in Q1 (Samsung and Micron 21% each, Counterpoint). Policy is now a supply variable: President Lee Jae Myung is pushing for a doubling of Korean memory output within five years backed by a $22bn support package, which — if executed alongside the four-fab Yongin cluster (first fab in production February) — is the strongest medium-term argument against sustained HBM/DRAM pricing power and cuts against 000660 and MU. The demand-side anecdotes (Huang's handwritten "Please make more" wafer, Counterpoint's MS Hwang noting every hotel near the Korean fabs is booked with Big Tech contract teams) are vivid but are sentiment, not order book. Watch whether state-subsidized capacity translates into announced wafer starts on a 2027–28 timeline; that is the datapoint that would turn the current price-deceleration story into a genuine oversupply call.
  Sources: [diariobitcoin.com](https://diariobitcoin.com/tecnologia/sk-hynix-apuesta-usd-720-000-millones-la-mayor-construccion-de-fabricas-de-memoria-para-ia)
- `4` **Manus to return as independent company after China forced Meta to unwind $2 billion deal**
  Manus will "soon return to operating as an independent company" after China's NDRC in April ordered Meta to unwind its $2bn December acquisition, completing a dismantling that began in June; users must back up data created on/after Dec 29, 2025. Concretely, Meta loses the general-purpose agent asset it intended to embed in consumer and enterprise products, and has fallen back on internally built tooling (Muse Code, launched last week) to compete with Anthropic/OpenAI — a setback for the meta-ai monetization thesis, though the text gives no financial recovery/write-off figure. The bigger read is regulatory: Beijing is now running a mirror-image CFIUS, blocking outbound tech/talent deals, which raises the cost of buying AI capability across borders and directly rhymes with the tiktok-ban question (both jurisdictions now treat algorithms/agents as strategic assets subject to forced divestiture). Confirm the transmission if Meta discloses an impairment or if Beijing blocks another cross-border AI deal; refute if Meta retains licensed access to Manus technology post-separation, which would make this cosmetic.
  Sources: [cnbc.com](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/08/11/manus-china-meta-acquisition.html)

  Live Nation's Q2 shows revenue +9% with fan attendance +10% and deferred revenue +25% YoY to $6.4bn, the latter being the forward-visibility metric that matters — it prepays H2 show economics and implies the 2026 tour cycle is booked, not hoped for. Venue Nation's owned-venue pipeline of >25 large venues is the margin lever (higher monetization per fan) running through 2027, while Ticketmaster and Sponsorship deliver the high-incremental-margin, non-admissions AOI. This is an author's Buy thesis rather than new company disclosure, so treat the AOI trajectory as inference; deferred revenue and attendance are the hard numbers. Confirm with next quarter's deferred revenue growth staying double-digit and per-fan monetization at owned venues; refute if attendance growth decelerates while deferred revenue flattens, which would signal the consumer-demand backdrop is rolling over.

- `2` **ON Holding: Great Results, Greater Expectations (Rating Upgrade)**
  On Holding cut FY revenue growth guidance from 23% to ~20% despite a strong Q2, and the stock fell ~20% — a roughly 3-point guide cut priced as a multiple reset, which is the classic penalty for a high-multiple compounder whose growth premium is the whole story. Offsetting: DTC mix drove gross margin to 65.4% and net margin to 12.4%, with a net cash balance, so the quality of earnings improved even as the growth rate decelerated; the author upgrades Sell→Hold on that basis. Growth now depends disproportionately on APAC expansion, which is the single variable to watch. Confirm/refute at Q3: APAC constant-currency growth holding and no further guidance trim would argue the cut was conservatism; a second cut with GM slipping below ~65% would confirm the deceleration is demand-driven, not phasing. *(headline only)*
